\int_{B}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\big|p_{\bar{\eta}(z)}^{*}(x\,|\,z)-p^{*}(x\,|\,z)\big|dxdz\\
\leq\int_{B}|\bar{\eta}(z)|V(\epsilon^{*})^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{J+2}|d_{i}(\epsilon^{*})|\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon^{*}}(x_{i})}k_{i}(x)dx}_{=V(\epsilon^{*})}dz\leq\sup_{z\in B}|\bar{\eta}(z)|\bar{d}\,\mathrm{Vol}\,(B)\leq\zeta.

(51)

Now define the density

p^{\dagger}(x\,|\,z):=p_{\bar{\eta}(z)}^{*}(x\,|\,z)=\begin{cases}p^{*}(x\,|\,z)+\bar{\eta}(z)\,V(\epsilon^{*})^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{J+2}d_{i}(\epsilon^{*})k_{i}(x)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon^{*}}(x_{i})}(x)&\quad\text{if }z\in B\\
p^{*}(x\,|\,z)&\quad\text{if }z\in\mathcal{Z}\backslash B\end{cases}.

Then for every
z\in B

\frac{\int_{\mathcal{X}}p^{\dagger}(x\,|\,z)W(x,z)U(x,z)dx}{\int_{\mathcal{X}}p^{\dagger}(x\,|\,z)W(x,z)dx}-\frac{\int_{\mathcal{X}}p^{*}(x\,|\,z)W(x,z)U(x,z)dx}{\int_{\mathcal{X}}p^{*}(x\,|\,z)W(x,z)dx}=\kappa,

proving
(i)
of the lemma.
By the construction in part 3
(ii)
follows immediately, and as
\bar{\eta}
is continuous
(iii)
follows. Finally
(iv)
follows by (
51
).
Thus all conditions for the lemma have been verified, completing the proof.

∎

Lemma 9
.

Consider the setup of Lemma
8
(alongside the definitions and notation within its proof). Then there exists some
\epsilon_{2}>0
such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\text{\emph{For Case 1: }}\sum_{i=1}^{J+2}\bigg|d_{i}(\epsilon)V_{i}(\epsilon)^{-1}\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon^{*}}(x_{i})}}k_{i}(x)\Delta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(x)dx-d_{i}(0)\Delta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(x_{i})dx\bigg|\leq 2^{-1}\omega,

(52)

\displaystyle\text{\emph{For Case 2: }}\sum_{i=1}^{J+2}\left|d_{i}(\epsilon)V_{i}(\epsilon)^{-1}\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon^{*}}(x_{i})}}k_{i}(x)\bar{W}_{0}^{\perp}(x)dx-d_{i}(0)\bar{W}_{0}^{\perp}(x_{i})\right|\leq 2^{-1}\omega.

(53)

Proof.

These results follow by straightforward continuity arguments, included below for completeness.
Recall that
\Delta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(x):=a_{0}\bar{r}_{0}^{\perp}(x)-(\bar{\beta}_{0}+c_{0})W_{0}(x)
is a continuous bounded function, and so for each
i\in[J+2]
the map
\epsilon\mapsto V_{i}(\epsilon)^{-1}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon}(x_{i})}k_{i}(x)\Delta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(x)dx
is continuous on the domain
[0,\epsilon_{1}]
, with
\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow 0}V_{i}(\epsilon)^{-1}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon}(x_{i})}k_{i}(x)\Delta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(x)dx=\Delta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(x_{i})
. Similarly the map
\epsilon\mapsto d_{i}(\epsilon)
is continuous on the domain
[0,\epsilon_{1}]
, and thus their product is continuous. Then there exists some
\epsilon_{2a}\in(0,\epsilon_{1}]
such that for each
i\in[J+2]

\bigg|d_{i}(\epsilon)V_{i}(\epsilon)^{-1}\int_{{\mathcal{B}_{\epsilon^{*}}(x_{i})}}k_{i}(x)\Delta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(x)dx-d_{i}(0)\Delta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(x_{i})dx\bigg|\leq 2^{-1}(J+2)^{-1}\omega,

and thus (
52
) follows for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{2a}]
. By continuity of
\bar{W}_{0}^{\perp}
it can similarly be shown that there exists some
\epsilon_{2b}\in(0,\epsilon_{1}]
such that (
53
) holds for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{2b}]
.
Taking
\epsilon_{2}:=\epsilon_{2a}\wedge\epsilon_{2b}
completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 10
.

Adopt the setup of Lemma
8
, specifically recalling that
W
,
U
and
(x,z)\mapsto p^{*}(x\,|\,z)
are bounded and equicontinuous in
z
. Then the functions

z\mapsto a(z):=\int_{\mathcal{X}}p^{*}(x\,|\,z)W(x,z)dx,\quad z\mapsto b(z):=\int_{\mathcal{X}}p^{*}(x\,|\,z)W(x,z)U(x,z)dx,

are continuous.

Proof.

Fix some
z\in\mathcal{Z}
and
\epsilon>0
. By equicontinuity of
W
there exists some
\delta_{1}>0
such that
|W(x,z^{\prime})-W(x,z)|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}
whenever
|z^{\prime}-z|\leq\delta_{1}
. Further, there exists a
\delta_{2}>0
and compact set
\mathcal{A}
such that
\int_{\mathcal{X}\backslash\mathcal{A}}p^{*}(x\,|\,z)dx\leq\frac{\epsilon}{6\|W\|_{\infty}}
for any
z\in\{z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}:|z^{\prime}-z|<\delta_{2}\}
. Also define
\mathrm{Vol}\,(\mathcal{A}):=\int_{\mathcal{A}}dx
. Then by equicontinuity of
(x,z)\mapsto p^{*}(x\,|\,z)
there exists some
\delta_{3}>0
such that
|p(x\,|\,z)-p(x\,|\,z^{\prime})|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3\|W\|_{\infty}\mathrm{Vol}\,(\mathcal{A})}
whenever
|z^{\prime}-z|<\delta_{3}
. Defining
\delta_{4}:=\delta_{1}\wedge\delta_{2}\wedge\delta_{3}
it follows that if
|z^{\prime}-z|<\delta_{4}
then

\displaystyle|a(z^{\prime})-a(z)|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\int_{\mathcal{X}}p^{*}(x\,|\,z)\big(W(x,z)-W(x,z^{\prime})\big)+W(x,z^{\prime})\big(p^{*}(x\,|\,z)-p^{*}(x\,|\,z^{\prime})\big)dx\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{X}}p^{*}(x\,|\,z)\big|W(x,z)-W(x,z^{\prime})\big|dx+\|W\|_{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\big|p^{*}(x\,|\,z)-p^{*}(x\,|\,z^{\prime})\big|dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}+\|W\|_{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{A}}\big|p^{*}(x\,|\,z)-p^{*}(x\,|\,z)\big|dx+\|W\|_{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{X}\backslash\mathcal{A}}p^{*}(x\,|\,z)dx

\displaystyle\hskip 200.0003pt+\|W\|_{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{X}\backslash\mathcal{A}}p^{*}(x\,|\,z^{\prime})dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}+\frac{\epsilon}{3}+\frac{\epsilon}{6}+\frac{\epsilon}{6}

\displaystyle=\epsilon,

and thus
a
is continuous. The function
b
can be shown to be continuous by identical arguments.
∎

Lemma 11
.

Suppose the functions
f_{1},\ldots,f_{n}:\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{R}
are linearly independent. Then there exist distinct
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in\mathcal{X}
such that the matrix

\mathbf{F}_{n}:=\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}(x_{1})&\cdots&f_{n}(x_{1})\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
f_{1}(x_{n})&\cdots&f_{n}(x_{n})\end{pmatrix}

is invertible.

Proof.

We use induction on
n
. Clearly the result is true when
n=1
, as we can take
x_{1}
to be any value such that
f_{1}(x_{1})\neq 0
;
f_{1}
cannot be the zero function, nor can
\mathcal{X}
be the empty set, as this would contradict the set
\{f_{1}\}
being linearly independent. Suppose now that the result holds for some
n\geq 1
; we shall show it holds at
n+1
. Given linearly independent functions
f_{1},\ldots,f_{n+1}
, we first use the induction hypothesis to pick distinct points
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
such that the corresponding matrix
\mathbf{F}_{n}
is invertible. Suppose, for a contradiction, that for all
x
,

\mathbf{F}_{n+1}(x):=\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}(x_{1})&\cdots&f_{n}(x_{1})&f_{n+1}(x_{1})\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
f_{1}(x_{n})&\cdots&f_{n}(x_{n})&f_{n+1}(x_{n})\\
f_{1}(x)&\cdots&f_{n}(x)&f_{n+1}(x)\end{pmatrix}

is singular. Then writing
\mathbf{c}:=\mathbf{F}_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{f}_{n+1}
, where
\mathbf{f}_{n+1}=(f_{n+1}(x_{1}),\ldots,f_{n+1}(x_{n}))^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we have that

c_{1}f_{1}(x)+\cdots+c_{n}f_{n}(x)=f_{n+1}(x)

for all
x\in\mathcal{X}
, contradicting the linear independence of
f_{1},\ldots,f_{n+1}
. Thus there exists an
x_{n+1}
such that
\mathbf{F}_{n+1}:=\mathbf{F}_{n+1}(x_{n+1})
is invertible. Moreover, we must have that
x_{n+1}\notin\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}
, as otherwise
\mathbf{F}_{n+1}
would have two identical rows.
∎

Lemma 12

(
Györfi et al., (1998)
, Lemma 3.1, adapted)
.

Let
b\in(0,1]
be some constant. Let
(a_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a non-increasing sequence of positive numbers converging to zero with
a_{1}\leq\alpha
for some constant
0<\alpha<b
. Then there exists a sequence
(p_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
of non-negative numbers satisfying

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}p_{k}=b,\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}p_{k}\Big(1-\frac{p_{k}}{b}\Big)^{n}\geq a_{n}\;\text{ for all }n\in\mathbb{N}.

Proof.

A proof for the case of
(b,\alpha)=(1,\frac{1}{2})
is given by
Györfi et al., (1998)
, Lemma 3.1 (we require a simple extension, included for completeness).
Let
p_{1}=b(1-\frac{a_{1}}{\alpha})
, and define the constant
A:=\frac{2b\log(\alpha^{-1}b)}{2+\log(\alpha^{-1}b)}\in(0,2b)
.
Then there exist integers
(l_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
1=l_{1}<l_{2}<\cdots
and
(p_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

p_{k}\leq\frac{A}{n},

for all
k>l_{n}
and

\sum_{k=l_{n}+1}^{m_{n+1}}p_{k}=\frac{b}{\alpha}(a_{n}-a_{n+1}).

Thus

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}p_{k}=p_{1}+\frac{b}{\alpha}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}(a_{i}-a_{i+1})=b,

and, for all
n\geq 2
,

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}p_{k}\Big(1-\frac{p_{k}}{b}\Big)^{n}\geq\left(1-\frac{A}{bn}\right)^{n}\sum_{k:p_{k}\leq\frac{A}{n}}p_{k}\geq e^{-\frac{2A}{2b-A}}\sum_{k:p_{k}\leq\frac{A}{n}}p_{k}\\
\geq e^{-\frac{2A}{2b-A}}\sum_{k=l_{n}+1}^{\infty}p_{k}=\frac{b}{\alpha}e^{-\frac{2A}{2b-A}}\sum_{i=n}^{\infty}(a_{i}-a_{i+1})=\frac{b}{\alpha}e^{-\frac{2A}{2b-A}}a_{n}=a_{n},

where the final equality follows as
A=\frac{2b\log(\alpha^{-1}b)}{2+\log(\alpha^{-1}b)}
.
∎

Lemma 13
.

Let
(x_{n,m})_{n\in\mathbb{N},m\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(y_{n,m})_{n\in\mathbb{N},m\in\mathbb{N}}
be sequences of non-negative numbers. Suppose also
\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}y_{n,m}\geq 1
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and for any subsequence
(m_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of the natural numbers
\limsup_{n\to\infty}x_{n,m_{n}}\geq 1
.
Then

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}x_{n,m}y_{n,m}\geq 1.

Proof.

Fix some
\epsilon\in(0,1)
. For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
there exists some
m^{\prime}_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

y_{n,m^{\prime}_{n}}\geq\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}y_{n,m}-\epsilon\geq 1-\epsilon,

where the final inequality follows by assumption. Thus

x_{n,m^{\prime}_{n}}y_{n,m^{\prime}_{n}}\geq(1-\epsilon)x_{n,m^{\prime}_{n}},

and so

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}x_{n,m}y_{n,m}\geq\limsup_{n\to\infty}x_{n,m^{\prime}_{n}}y_{n,m^{\prime}_{n}}\geq(1-\epsilon)\limsup_{n\to\infty}x_{n,m^{\prime}_{n}}\geq 1-\epsilon,

where the final inequality follows by assumption.
Taking
\epsilon\downarrow 0
completes the proof.
∎

Appendix D
Supplementary Information for Section
3

In Appendix
D.1
we prove the uniform asymptotic Gaussainity result for the weighted
\theta
estimator (Theorem
3
). We conclude the section by exploring the doubly robust condition of
(M2)
in more detail, showing in Appendix
D.2
that in some models we can accommodate slower nuisance convergence regimes than those given by (
22
).

D.1
Proof of Theorem
3

Proof of Theorem
3
.

The proof consists of three main parts, showing: consistency of
\hat{\theta}
; asymptotic normality of
\hat{\theta}
; and consistency of the estimator
\hat{V}
for the asymptotic variance
V^{*}_{P}
, all of which will be shown to hold uniformly over
P\in\mathcal{P}
(for the class of distributions
\mathcal{P}
defined through Assumption
A1
). For notational ease we will suppress the dependence of
\eta
on the covariates
Z
, and so will notate e.g.
\psi_{j}(S;\theta,\eta(Z))
and
\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta,\eta(Z))
as
\psi_{j}(S;\theta,\eta)
and
\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta,\eta)
respectively throughout. For avoidance of doubt, for any
q\in\mathbb{N}
quantities such as
\psi_{j}^{q}(S;\theta,\eta(Z))
should be read as
\{\psi_{j}(S;\theta,\eta(Z))\}^{q}
. We also notate
S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}:=(S_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}
and, as in Algorithm
1

\hat{\eta}
is estimated using
S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}
conditioning on
S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}
can be read as equivalent to conditioning on
\hat{\eta}

Uniform Consistency:

\hat{\theta}=\theta_{P}+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)

By Lemma
26
(a simple extension of Theorem 5.9 of
van der Vaart, (1998)
for uniform convergence over
\mathcal{P}
) alongside Assumption
(M1)
, it suffices to show that

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\psi(S_{i};\theta,\hat{\eta}^{(k)},\hat{w}^{(k)})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\psi(S_{i};\theta,\eta_{P},w^{*}_{P})\right]\bigg|=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1).

(54)

To show this, we decompose

\displaystyle\quad\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\psi(S_{i};\theta,\hat{\eta}^{(k)},\hat{w}^{(k)})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\psi(S_{i};\theta,\eta_{P},w^{*}_{P})\right]\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\big\{\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta,\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta,\eta_{P})\right]\big\}\bigg|.

Then

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\big\{\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta,\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta,\eta_{P})\right]\big\}\bigg|\,\bigg|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big|\cdot\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta,\eta_{P})|\,\Big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big|\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta,\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta,\eta_{P})\big|\,\Big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right],

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big)^{2}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big(\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta,\eta_{P})\big)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{Kc_{w}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big|\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta,\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta,\eta_{P})\big|\,\Big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

using the given assumption on convergence of
\hat{w}^{(k)}
, Assumption
(M3)
and Lemma
14
. Therefore (
54
) follows, and thus

\hat{\theta}=\theta_{P}+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1).

Uniform Asymptotic Normality:

We decompose the empirical
\psi
function as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\psi(S_{i};\hat{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)},\hat{w}^{(k)})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P},w^{*}_{P})

(55)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\underbrace{\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\big\{\psi_{j}(S_{i};\hat{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big\}}_{=:A_{\textup{I},j}^{(k)}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\underbrace{\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\big\{\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big\}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\big]}_{=:A_{\textup{II},j}^{(k)}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\underbrace{\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z)\big)\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})}_{=:A_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\underbrace{\sqrt{\frac{K}{n}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{\mathds{G}}_{P}^{(k)}\Big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\big\{\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big\}\Big)}_{=:A_{\textup{IV},j}^{(k)}},

where the empirical process term is defined as

\hat{\mathds{G}}_{P}^{(k)}(g(S))=\sqrt{\frac{K}{n}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\Big(g(S_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{P}[g(S_{i})\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}]\Big),

The first and second terms in this decomposition will dominate the other terms, and contribute to the asymptotic Gaussianity results. We first show that the terms indexed by
\textup{II},\textup{III}
and
IV
are all
o_{\mathcal{P}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
.

For the term
A_{\textup{II}}:=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}A_{\textup{II},j}^{(k)}
,

\displaystyle\big|A_{\textup{II},j}^{(k)}\big|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\nabla_{\eta(z)}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\,|\,Z_{i}\big]^{\top}\big(\hat{\eta}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-\eta_{P}(Z_{i})\big)\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{K}{2n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\big(\hat{\eta}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-\eta_{P}(Z_{i})\big)^{\top}\hat{H}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i};\tilde{r})\big(\hat{\eta}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-\eta_{P}(Z_{i})\big)\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\Big]\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Kc_{w}}{2n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\Big|\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\sup_{r\in[0,1]}\big(\hat{\eta}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-\eta_{P}(Z_{i})\big)^{\top}\hat{H}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i};r)\big(\hat{\eta}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-\eta_{P}(Z_{i})\big)\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}\big(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big).

for some
\tilde{r}\in[0,1]
, following by ‘Neyman orthogonality’ i.e.
\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P})
is orthogonal to the nuisance tangent space of the model;

\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\nabla_{\eta(z)}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\,|\,Z\big]=0,

which holds under the partially parametric model (Theorem
1
), and ‘double robustness’ (Assumption
(M2)
). Recall that

\hat{H}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i};r):=\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\nabla_{\eta(z)}^{2}\psi_{j}\big(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z_{i})+r(\hat{\eta}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-\eta_{P}(Z_{i}))\big)\,|\,Z_{i},S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\big]

.

For
A_{\textup{III}}:=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}A_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)}
, first note that as
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\,|\,Z_{i}]=0
we have that
\mathbb{E}_{P}[A_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)}\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}]=0
. Also

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\big(\sqrt{n}A_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)}\big)^{2}\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K^{2}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big)^{2}\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K^{2}2^{\frac{4}{2+\delta}}c_{w}^{\frac{4}{2+\delta}}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big)^{\frac{2\delta}{2+\delta}}\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K^{2}2^{\frac{4}{2+\delta}}c_{w}^{\frac{4}{2+\delta}}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big)^{2}\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\big]^{\frac{\delta}{2+\delta}}}_{=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)\text{ as in the statement of the theorem}}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[|\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})|^{2+\delta}\big]^{\frac{2}{2+\delta}}}_{=O_{\mathcal{P}}(1)\text{ by Assumption~\ref{ass:bounded-moments}}}

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

by Hölder’s inequality. Thus
A_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
and subsequently
A_{\textup{III}}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
.

For the
A_{\textup{IV}}:=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}A_{\textup{IV},j}^{(k)}
term, first note that
\mathbb{E}[A_{\textup{IV},j}^{(k)}\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}]=0
, and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\big(\sqrt{n}A_{\textup{IV},j}^{(k)}\big)^{2}\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K^{2}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\big)^{2}\big\{\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big\}^{2}\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K^{2}c_{w}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\big\{\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big\}^{2}\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\big]

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

thus
A_{\textup{IV},j}^{(k)}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
and so
A_{\textup{IV}}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
.

For the
A_{\textup{I}}:=\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}A_{\textup{I},j}^{(k)}
term we perform a second order Taylor expansion in
\theta
about
\theta_{P}
,

\displaystyle A_{\textup{I},j}^{(k)}

\displaystyle=\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\big\{\psi_{j}(S_{i};\hat{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big\}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\bigg)\big(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg(\frac{K}{2n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\nabla_{\theta}^{2}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\bar{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\bigg)\big(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}_{j}^{(k)}\big(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}\big),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{j}^{(k)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})+\Big(\frac{K}{2n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\nabla_{\theta}^{2}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\bar{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\Big)\big(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}\big)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}[w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})]}_{=:I_{j}^{(k)}>0}

(56)

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\big\{\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big\}}_{=:A_{\textup{I}a,j}^{(k)}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big)\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})}_{=:A_{\textup{I}b,j}^{(k)}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\big\{w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})-\mathbb{E}_{P}[w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})]\big\}}_{=:A_{\textup{I}c,j}^{(k)}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\bigg(\frac{K}{2n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\nabla_{\theta}^{2}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\bar{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\bigg)\big(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}\big)}_{=:A_{\textup{I}d,j}^{(k)}},

for some
\bar{\theta}\in[(\hat{\theta}\wedge\theta_{P})\,,\,(\hat{\theta}\vee\theta_{P})]
. For the term
A_{\textup{I}a,j}^{(k)}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[|A_{\textup{I}a,j}^{(k)}|\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[|\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})|\cdot\big|\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big|\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Kc_{w}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\big|\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big|\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\big]

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

by Lemma
14
. Also,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[|A_{\textup{I}b,j}^{(k)}|\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\big|\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big|\cdot\big|\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big|\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big)^{2}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\big(\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big)^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

using the assumption in the statement of Theorem
3
(holding e.g. for ROSE random forest generated weights by Theorem
5
), and so
A_{\textup{I}b,j}^{(k)}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
.
Also, note that
\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[A_{\textup{I}c,j}^{(k)}\big]=0
and

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}\big[\big(A_{\textup{I}c,j}^{(k)}\big)^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K^{2}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\big(w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big)^{2}\big(\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K^{2}c_{w}^{2}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\big(\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=o(1).

Finally, for the term
A_{\textup{I}d,j}^{(k)}
note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\bigg|\frac{K}{2n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\nabla_{\theta}^{2}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\bar{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\bigg|\,\Bigg|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Kc_{w}}{2n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big|\nabla_{\theta}^{2}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta,\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big|\,\Big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle=O_{\mathcal{P}}(1).

This, in conjunction with
\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
gives
A_{\textup{I}d,j}^{(k)}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
.

Combining all these results gives
A_{\textup{I}a,j}^{(k)}+A_{\textup{I}b,j}^{(k)}+A_{\textup{I}c,j}^{(k)}+A_{\textup{I}d,j}^{(k)}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
and thus, recalling
I_{j}^{(k)}
as defined in (
56
),

A_{\textup{I},j}^{(k)}=\big(I_{j}^{(k)}+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)\big)\big(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}\big),

and thus (recalling both
J
and
K
are finite)

\displaystyle A_{\textup{I}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}I_{j}^{(k)}+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)\bigg)\big(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}\big)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big]+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)\bigg)\big(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}\big).

Further noting that
\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\,|\,Z\big]\allowbreak=\allowbreak\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\,|\,Z\big]
, the decomposition (
55
) becomes

o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P},w^{*}_{P})=\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{P}[w_{P,j}^{*}(Z)\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P})]+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)\bigg)\cdot\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}\big).

Upon rearranging we have

\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}\big)=\frac{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P},w^{*}_{P})+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)}{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{P}[w_{P,j}^{*}(Z)\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P})]+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)},

noting in particular the non-singularity assumption (
23
);

\inf_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{P}[w_{P,j}^{*}(Z)\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P})]=\inf_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{P}[w_{P,j}^{*}(Z)\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P})]>0

The result then follows by Lemmas
23
and
24
;

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{P}\left(\sqrt{n/V^{*}_{P}}\big(\hat{\theta}_{\hat{w}}-\theta_{P}\big)\leq t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|=0,

for
V^{*}_{P}
as in the statement of the theorem.

Consistency of
\hat{V}
:

We finally must show that, for
\hat{V}
as in Algorithm
1
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{P}\left(\sqrt{n/\hat{V}}\big(\hat{\theta}_{\hat{w}}-\theta_{P}\big)\leq t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|=0.

By Lemma
24
it sufficies to show

\hat{V}=V^{*}_{P}+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1).

The estimator
\hat{V}
in Algorithm
1
can be decomposed as

\displaystyle\hat{V}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\big)^{2}\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\hat{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})}{\left(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\hat{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[w_{P,j}^{*}(Z)\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\right]+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\Big(B_{\textup{I},j}+\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\big(B_{\textup{II},j}^{(k)}+B_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)}+B_{\textup{IV},j}^{(k)}\big)\Big)\right)^{-2}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\left(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[w^{*2}(Z)\psi_{j}^{2}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\right]+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\Big(C_{\textup{I},j}+\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\big(C_{\textup{II},j}^{(k)}+C_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)}+C_{\textup{IV},j}^{(k)}\big)\Big)\right),

(57)

where

\displaystyle B_{\textup{I},j}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})-\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[w^{*}_{P}(Z)\partial_{\theta}\psi(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\right]\big)

\displaystyle B_{\textup{II},j}^{(k)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big)\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})

\displaystyle B_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\big\{\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big\}

\displaystyle B_{\textup{IV},j}^{(k)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\big\{\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\hat{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big\}

\displaystyle C_{\textup{I},j}

\displaystyle:=\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\big(w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big)^{2}\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})-\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[w_{P,j}^{*2}(X)\psi_{j}^{2}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\right]\Big)

\displaystyle C_{\textup{II},j}^{(k)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\Big(\big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\big)^{2}-\big(w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big)^{2}\Big)\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})

\displaystyle C_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\big)^{2}\big\{\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big\}

\displaystyle C_{\textup{IV},j}^{(k)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})\big)^{2}\big\{\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\hat{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big\}.

We proceed by showing all of these terms are
o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
for all
(j,k)\in[J]\times[K]
.

For the term
B_{\textup{I},j}
, note that

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big(B_{\textup{I},j}\big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K^{2}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big(w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})-\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\right]\big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K^{2}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big(w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big)^{2}\big(\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K^{2}c_{w}^{2}}{n}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big(\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big)^{2}\right]=O(n^{-1}K)=o(1),

by Assumptions
(M3)
and
(M4)
. Therefore by Markov’s inequality
B_{\textup{I}}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
.

Consider the term
B_{\textup{II},j}^{(k)}
. By the triangle and Cauchy–Schwarz inequalities,

\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|B_{\textup{II},j}^{(k)}\big|\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]\leq\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg[\Big|\big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big)\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\Big|\,\Big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\bigg]\\
\leq\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big)^{2}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big(\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

using Assumption
(M3)
and Theorem
5
, and so
B_{\textup{II},j}^{(k)}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
.

For the term
B_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)}
,

\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|B_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)}\big|\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]\leq\frac{Kc_{w}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i},\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i},\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big|\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

by Lemma
14
. Again,
B_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
follows by Markov’s inequality.

For the term
B_{\textup{IV},j}^{(k)}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\big|B_{\textup{IV},j}^{(k)}\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Kc_{w}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\hat{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Kc_{w}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\Big(\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big|\nabla_{\theta}\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta,\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big|\Big)\cdot|\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Kc_{w}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\Big(\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big|\nabla_{\theta}\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta,\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big|\Big)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}\big)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{Kc_{w}}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\Big(\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big|\nabla_{\theta}\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta,\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big|\Big)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[n\big(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}\big)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=O(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

by Lemma
25
, and thus by Markov’s inequality
B_{\textup{IV},j}^{(k)}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
.

We show
C_{\textup{I}}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
separately for the two cases
\delta\geq 2
and
\delta\in(0,2)
. If
\delta\geq 2
then

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[C_{\textup{I},j}^{2}\right]\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big(w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big)^{4}\psi_{j}^{4}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\right]\\
\leq\frac{c_{w}^{4}}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\psi_{j}^{4}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\right]=O(n^{-1}),

by Assumptions
(M3)
and
(M4)
, with the result then following by Markov’s Theorem. If however
\delta\in(0,2)
then by the von–Bahr–Esseen inequality

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\left|C_{\textup{I},j}\right|^{1+\frac{\delta}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=n^{-\left(1+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big)^{2}\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(2-n^{-1}\right)n^{-\left(1+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[w_{P,j}^{*(2+\delta)}(Z_{i})\left|\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\right|^{2+\delta}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2M^{4+2\delta}n^{-\left(1+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\left|\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\right|^{2+\delta}\right]=O\big(n^{-\frac{\delta}{2}}\big).

In either case, the result
C_{\textup{I},j}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
follows for any
\delta>0
.

For the term
C_{\textup{II},j}^{(k)}
, again consider the cases
\delta\geq 2
and
\delta\in(0,2)
separately. If
\delta\geq 2

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|C_{\textup{II},j}^{(k)}\big|\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})+w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big|\cdot\big|\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big|\cdot\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2Kc_{w}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big|\big(\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big)^{2}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2Kc_{w}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big(\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big)^{2}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\psi_{j}^{4}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

using the triangle inequality, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, and the ROSE random forest consistency result of Theroem
5
.
If on the other hand
\delta\in(0,2)
then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|C_{\textup{II},j}^{(k)}\big|\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2Kc_{w}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big|\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2Kc_{w}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big|^{2+\frac{2-\delta}{\delta}}\,\Big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]^{\frac{\delta}{2+\delta}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\left|\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\right|^{2+\delta}\right]^{\frac{2}{2+\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K\left(2c_{w}\right)^{\frac{4}{2+\delta}}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|\hat{w}_{j}^{(k)}(Z_{i})-w_{P,j}^{*}(Z_{i})\big|^{2}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]^{\frac{\delta}{2+\delta}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\left|\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\right|^{2+\delta}\right]^{\frac{2}{2+\delta}}

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

by the triangle inequality, Hölder’s inequality, Assumptions
(M3)
and
(M4)
and Theorem
5
.

For the term
C_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|C_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)}\big|\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Kc_{w}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big|\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2Kc_{w}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big(\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big)^{2}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big\{\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big\}^{2}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{Kc_{w}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big\{\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big\}^{2}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and Lemma
14
. Therefore it follows that
\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[|C_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)}|\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\big]=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
and thus
C_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
.

For the
C_{\textup{IV},j}^{(k)}
term, first note

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|C_{\textup{IV},j}^{(k)}\big|\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Kc_{w}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\hat{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big|\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2Kc_{w}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\psi_{j}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big\{\psi_{j}(S_{i};\hat{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big\}^{2}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{Kc_{w}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big\{\psi_{j}(S_{i};\hat{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big\}^{2}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right],

\displaystyle=\frac{Kc_{w}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big\{\psi_{j}(S_{i};\hat{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big\}^{2}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

by Lemma
14
. Further,

\displaystyle\quad\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big\{\psi_{j}(S_{i};\hat{\theta},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\Big(\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big|\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta,\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big|\Big)^{2}\big(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}\big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\Big(\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big|\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta,\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big|\Big)^{2+\delta}\right]^{\frac{1}{2+\delta}}\cdot\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\big(\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}\big)\big)^{\frac{2(2+\delta)}{\delta}}\Big]^{\frac{\delta}{2+\delta}}

\displaystyle=o(1),

by Hölder’s inequality, Assumptions
(M3)
and the asymptotic normality result of Theorem
3
alongside Lemma
25
.

In summary, we have shown
B_{\textup{I},j},B_{\textup{II},j}^{(k)},B_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)},B_{\textup{IV},j}^{(k)},C_{\textup{I},j},C_{\textup{II},j}^{(k)},C_{\textup{III},j}^{(k)},C_{\textup{IV},j}^{(k)}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
, and thus

\hat{V}=V^{*}_{P}+o_{\mathcal{P}}(1).

Applying a uniform version of Slutsky’s Theorem (Lemma
24
) to the decomposition (
57
) completes the proof;

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{P}\left(\sqrt{n/\hat{V}}\big(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{P}\big)\leq t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|=0,

∎

D.1.1
Auxiliary lemmas for the asymptotic Gaussianity results

Lemma 14
.

Under Assumption
A1
(and adopting the notation and setup of Theorems
3
and
5
) the following hold for each
j\in[J]
:

(i)

\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|\psi_{j}(S;\theta,\hat{\eta})-\psi_{j}(S;\theta,\eta_{P})|\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
,

(ii)

\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\big\{\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\hat{\eta})-\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P})|\big\}^{2}\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
,

(iii)

\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\big|\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\hat{\eta})-\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P})|\big|\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
,

(iv)

\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[|\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\hat{\eta})-\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P})|\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
.

Proof.

We prove each statement of the lemma in turn. For (i), note first that for each
\theta\in\Theta
we have

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|\psi_{j}(S;\theta,\hat{\eta}(Z))-\psi_{j}(S;\theta,\eta_{P}(Z))|\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{r\in[0,1]}\big|\nabla_{\eta(z)}\psi_{j}\big(S;\theta,\eta_{P}(Z)+r(\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z))\big)^{\top}\big(\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z)\big)\big|\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{r\in[0,1]}\left\lVert\nabla_{\eta(z)}\psi_{j}\big(S;\theta,\eta_{P}(Z)+r(\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z))\big)\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\,|\,\hat{\eta}\Big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\left\lVert\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z)\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

by Assumptions
(M2)
and
(M3)
.

For (ii) we begin by defining
r_{ijk}:=\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})
,

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big\{\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})-\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big\}^{2}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[r_{ijk}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|r_{ijk}|>1\}}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[r_{ijk}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|r_{ijk}|\leq 1\}}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[|r_{ijk}|^{2+\delta}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]^{\frac{2}{2+\delta}}\mathbb{P}_{P}\left(|r_{ijk}|>1\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right)^{\frac{\delta}{2+\delta}}+\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[|r_{ijk}|\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[|r_{ijk}|^{2+\delta}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]^{\frac{2}{2+\delta}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[|r_{ijk}|\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]^{\frac{\delta}{2+\delta}}+\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[|r_{ijk}|\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2+\delta}\left(\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}^{(k)})\big|^{2+\delta}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|\psi_{j}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P})\big|^{2+\delta}\,\big|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]\right)^{\frac{2}{2+\delta}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[|r_{ijk}|\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]^{\frac{\delta}{2+\delta}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[|r_{ijk}|\,|\,S_{\mathcal{I}_{k}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

by Hölder’s inequality and Assumption
(M3)
.
Similarly, for (iii) we have that

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[|\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\hat{\eta}(Z))-\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))|\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\sup_{r\in[0,1]}\big|\nabla_{\eta(z)}\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}\big(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z)+r(\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z))\big)^{\top}\big(\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z)\big)\big|\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\sup_{r\in[0,1]}\left\lVert\nabla_{\eta(z)}\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}\big(S;\theta,\eta_{P}(Z)+r(\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z))\big)\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\,|\,\hat{\eta}\Big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\left\lVert\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z)\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

again by Assumptions
(M2)
and
(M3)
. The result of (iii) can be shown similarly.

∎

D.1.2
Relaxed regularity conditions

The regularity conditions of Assumption
A1
,
(M3)
dictates that there exists some
\delta>0
such that

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{r\in[0,1]]}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\big\|D\psi_{j}(S;\theta,\eta_{P}(Z)+r\{\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z)\})\big\|_{2}^{2+\delta}\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]=O_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

for all operators
D\in\{\text{id},\nabla_{\theta},\nabla_{\theta}^{2},\partial_{\theta}\}
. This condition is stronger than required for the results of Theorems
3
and
5
(and only relaxed for notational simplicity). In particular, such regularity conditions can be relaxed as follows; there exists some
\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta,\eta_{P}(Z))^{2}\big]=O(1),\quad\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[|\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))|^{2+\delta}\big]=O(1),

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))^{2}\big]=O(1),\quad\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))^{2}\big]=O(1),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|\nabla_{\theta}^{2}\psi_{j}(S;\theta,\eta_{P}(Z))|\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]=O_{\mathcal{P}}(1),\quad\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|\nabla_{\theta}^{2}\psi_{j}(S;\theta,\hat{\eta}(Z))|\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]=O_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta,\hat{\eta}(Z))|^{2+\delta}\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]=O_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{r\in[0,1]}\big\|\nabla_{\eta(z)}\psi_{j}\big(S;\theta,\eta_{P}(Z)+r(\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z))\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}\,\big|\,\hat{\eta}\Big]=O_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\sup_{r\in[0,1]}\big\|\nabla_{\eta(z)}\nabla_{\theta}\psi_{j}\big(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z)+r(\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z))\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}\,\big|\,\hat{\eta}\Big]=O_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\sup_{r\in[0,1]}\big\|\nabla_{\eta(z)}\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}\big(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z)+r(\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z))\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}\,\big|\,\hat{\eta}\Big]=O_{\mathcal{P}}(1).

D.2
Comments on sufficient conditions for doubly robust rates

We present relevant sufficient conditions for the DR property of Assumption
A1
,
(M2)
to hold. In particular, in the partially linear model studied in Section
1.1
with nuisance parameterisation
\eta_{P}=(f_{P},m_{P,1},\ldots,m_{P,J})
it is well known the DR condition
(M2)
holds when for each
j\in[J]

\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\big(\hat{m}_{j}(Z)-m_{P,j}(Z)\big)^{2}\,\Big|\,\hat{m}_{j}\Big]\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\big(\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\big)^{2}\,\Big|\,\hat{f}\Big]=o_{\mathcal{P}}\big(n^{-1}\big).

(58)

This form of double robustness only asks for the product of the mean squared errors of
\hat{m}_{j}
and
\hat{f}
to converge at faster than the rate
n^{-1}
. Rather, one of
m_{P,j}
and
f_{P}
may be estimated at relatively slow rates (e.g. with mean squared error converging at slower than
n^{-\frac{1}{2}}
rates), provided the other estimator compensates with faster rates.
We remark that for estimators in the partially parametric model of the form (
8
) with
\eta_{P}=(f_{P},m_{P,1},\ldots,m_{P,J})
(
58
) is not necessarily sufficient for the doubly robust rates of Assumption
(M2)
. We outline weaker sufficient conditions than those presented in Section
3.2
for the doubly robust rates of Assumption
(M2)
, that also encapsulates this stronger case (
58
) for the partially linear model. Whilst some of the conditions here appear rather involved, we later explain two simple examples of the generalised partially linear model where the conditions of Proposition
15
(a) are satisfied (see Corollary
16
).
In the following results for ease of exposition we introduce the following notation. Given an estimator
\hat{f}
and
r\in[0,1]
we write

\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z):=\mu(X,Z;\theta_{P},f_{P}(Z)+r\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\}).

We assume for all
(x,z)\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Z}
the map
\gamma\mapsto\mu(x,z;\theta,\gamma)
on the domain
\mathbb{R}
is continuously differentiable, and denote for each
q\in\mathbb{N}
,

\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(q)}(X,Z):=\frac{\partial^{q}\mu(X,Z;\theta_{P},\gamma)}{\partial\gamma^{q}}\bigg|_{\gamma=f_{P}(Z)+r\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\}}.

It will also help to define the functions

\displaystyle\varphi_{r}(X,Z)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\tilde{\mu}^{(2)}_{r}(X,Z)}{\tilde{\mu}^{(1)}_{r}(X,Z)}+\frac{2\{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(2)}(X,Z)\}^{2}-\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(3)}(X,Z)\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)}{\{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)\}^{3}}\big(\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z)-\tilde{\mu}_{0}(X,Z)\big),

\displaystyle\zeta_{r}(X,Z)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(2)}(X,Z)}{\{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)\}^{2}}\big(\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z)-\tilde{\mu}_{0}(X,Z)\big).

Note in particular
\varphi_{r}
and
\zeta_{r}
each carry dependence on
\hat{f}
.
We also take
C_{g},C_{\sigma}>0
to be finite positive constants. Note in the following conditional expectations if
\hat{f}
and
\hat{m}_{j}
are estimated using the same dataset then conditioning on
\hat{\eta}
,
\hat{f}
and
\hat{m}_{j}
are all equivalent.

Proposition 15
.

Consider the partially parametric model with
\gamma\mapsto\mu(x,z;\theta_{P},\gamma)
continuously differentiable for all
(x,z)\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Z}
, and with

\big|\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\varphi_{r}(X,Z)\,|\,\hat{f},Z\big]\big|\vee\big|\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\zeta_{r}(X,Z)\,|\,\hat{f},Z\big]\big|\leq C_{g},

almost surely.
Suppose also one of the following two cases holds:

(a)

For each
z\in\mathcal{Z}
and
r\in[0,1]
the map
x\mapsto\varphi_{r}(x,z)
on
\mathcal{X}
is constant.

(b)

\mathrm{Var}_{P}(M_{j}(X)\,|\,Z)\leq C_{\sigma}
almost surely, and
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\varphi_{r}(X,Z)^{2}\,|\,\hat{f},Z]\leq C_{g}^{2}
.

Suppose the nuisance estimators
\hat{\eta}=(\hat{m}_{1},\ldots,\hat{m}_{j},\hat{f})
satisfy for each
j\in[J]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\|\hat{m}_{j}(Z)-m_{P,j}(Z)\|^{4+\delta}\,|\,\hat{m}_{j}\big],\;\,\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\|\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\|^{4+\delta}\,|\,\hat{f}\big]=O_{\mathcal{P}}(1),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\big|\{\hat{m}_{j}(Z)-m_{P,j}(Z)\}\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\}\big|\,\big|\,\hat{\eta}\big]=o_{\mathcal{P}}\big(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big),

for some
\delta>0
.
Additionally, but only for case (b), suppose

\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip-123.76965pt(b)}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\}^{2}\,|\,\hat{f}\,\big]=o_{\mathcal{P}}\big(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big).

Then the DR property of Assumption
A1
,
(M2)
holds.

Proof.

Without loss of generality take
J=1
,
m_{P}:=m_{P,1}
and
\eta_{P}=(m_{P},f_{P})
.
Recall the function
\psi_{1}
takes the form

\psi_{1}\big(S;\theta,\eta(Z)\big):=\big(M_{1}(X)-m_{P}(Z)\big)\frac{Y-\mu(X,Z;\theta,\eta(Z))}{\mu^{\prime}(X,Z;\theta,\eta(Z))}.

Define

\displaystyle\tilde{\xi}_{r}(X,Z)

\displaystyle:=(M_{1}(X)-m_{P}(Z))-r\{\hat{m}(Z)-m_{P}(Z)\},

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle:=\tilde{\xi}_{0}(X,Z)=M_{1}(X)-m_{P}(Z),

\displaystyle\tilde{\epsilon}_{r}(S)

\displaystyle:=Y-\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z),

recalling
S:=(Y,X,Z)
, and noting we suppress a number of dependencies in the above definition e.g. on
\hat{\eta}
and
P
. Then direct calculations give

\nabla_{\eta(z)}^{2}\psi_{1}\big(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z)+r(\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z))\big)=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(2)}(X,Z)}{\big(\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)\big)^{2}}\tilde{\epsilon}_{r}(S)\\
+\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\bigg(\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(2)}(X,Z)}{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)}+\frac{2\{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(2)}(X,Z)\}^{2}-\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(3)}(X,Z)\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)}{\{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)\}^{3}}\tilde{\epsilon}_{r}(S)\bigg)\tilde{\xi}_{r}(X,Z).

Thus

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[0,1]}\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\{\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z)\}^{\top}\mathbb{E}\big[\nabla_{\eta(z)}^{2}\psi_{1}\big(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z)+r\{\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z)\}\big)\,|\,\hat{\eta},Z\big]\{\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z)\}\,\Big|\,\hat{\eta}\Big]

\displaystyle=\sup_{r\in[0,1]}\bigg\{2\,\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\{\hat{m}(Z)-m_{P}(Z)\}\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\}\,\big|\,\hat{\eta}\big]}_{=:R_{\textup{I}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+2\,\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg[\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(2)}(X,Z)}{\{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)\}^{2}}\tilde{\epsilon}_{r}(S)\{\hat{m}(Z)-m_{P}(Z)\}\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\}\,\bigg|\,\hat{\eta}\bigg]}_{=:R_{\textup{II}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(2)}(X,Z)}{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)}+\frac{2\{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(2)}(X,Z)\}^{2}-\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(3)}(X,Z)\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)}{\{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)\}^{3}}\tilde{\epsilon}_{r}(S)\bigg)\tilde{\xi}_{r}(X,Z)\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\}^{2}\,\bigg|\,\hat{\eta}\bigg]}_{=:R_{\textup{III}}}\bigg\}

The first term
R_{\textup{I}}
in this expansion is
o_{\mathcal{P}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
by assumption. For the second term
R_{\textup{II}}
,

\displaystyle\quad\bigg|\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg[\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(2)}(X,Z)}{\{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)\}^{2}}\tilde{\epsilon}_{r}(S)\,\bigg|\,\hat{\eta},Z\bigg]\bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg[\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(2)}(X,Z)}{\{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)\}^{2}}\Big(\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[Y-\tilde{\mu}_{0}(X,Z)\,|\,X,Z\big]}_{=0}-\big(\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z)-\tilde{\mu}_{0}(X,Z)\big)\Big)\,\bigg|\,\hat{f},Z\bigg]\bigg|

\displaystyle=\big|\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\zeta_{r}(X,Z)\,\big|\,\hat{f},Z\big]\big|\leq C_{g},

and so
R_{\textup{II}}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
in either case.

To control the term
R_{\textup{III}}
, first notice

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(2)}(X,Z)}{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)}+\frac{2\{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(2)}(X,Z)\}^{2}-\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(3)}(X,Z)\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)}{\{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)\}^{3}}\tilde{\epsilon}_{r}(S)\bigg)\tilde{\xi}_{r}(X,Z)\,\bigg|\,\hat{\eta},Z\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\varphi_{r}(X,Z)\tilde{\xi}_{r}(X,Z)\,\Big|\,\hat{\eta},Z\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg[\frac{2(\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(2)}(X,Z))^{2}-\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(3)}(X,Z)\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)}{\{\tilde{\mu}_{r}^{(1)}(X,Z)\}^{3}}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{P}[Y-\tilde{\mu}_{0}(X,Z)\,|\,X,Z]}_{=0}\tilde{\xi}_{r}(X,Z)\,\bigg|\,\hat{\eta},Z\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\varphi_{r}(X,Z)\tilde{\xi}_{r}(X,Z)\,\Big|\,\hat{\eta},Z\Big],

where the first inequality follows due to identity
\tilde{\epsilon}_{r}(S)=\{Y-\tilde{\mu}_{0}(X,Z)\}-\{\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z)-\tilde{\mu}_{0}(X,Z)\}
and by the definition of
\varphi_{r}
.
Therefore

\displaystyle R_{\textup{III}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\varphi_{r}(X,Z)\tilde{\xi}_{r}(X,Z)\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\}^{2}\,\Big|\,\hat{\eta}\Big]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\varphi_{r}(X,Z)\xi\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\}^{2}\,\Big|\,\hat{\eta}\Big]}_{=:R_{\textup{III}a}}

\displaystyle\qquad-r\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\varphi_{r}(X,Z)\{\hat{m}(Z)-m_{P}(Z)\}\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\}^{2}\,\Big|\,\hat{\eta}\Big]}_{=:R_{\textup{III}b}}.

For the latter term
R_{\textup{III}b}
,

\displaystyle|R_{\textup{III}b}|

\displaystyle=\big|\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\mathbb{E}_{P}[\varphi_{r}(X,Z)\,|\,\hat{f},Z]\{\hat{m}(Z)-m_{P}(Z)\}\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\}^{2}\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]\big|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[|\mathbb{E}_{P}[\varphi_{r}(X,Z)\,|\,\hat{f},Z]|\cdot|\hat{m}(Z)-m_{P}(Z)|\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\}^{2}\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{g}\,\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[|\hat{m}(Z)-m_{P}(Z)|\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\}^{2}\,|\,\hat{\eta}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{g}\,\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\{\hat{m}(Z)-m_{P}(Z)\}^{2}\,|\,\hat{m}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\}^{2}\,|\,\hat{f}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\}^{4+\delta}\,|\,\hat{f}\big]^{\frac{1}{2+\delta}}

\displaystyle=o_{\mathcal{P}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

where in the final inequality we make use of the following inequality: for non-negative real-valued random variables
A
and
B
,

\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[AB^{2}\big]\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[A^{\frac{2+\delta}{1+\delta}}B^{\frac{\delta}{1+\delta}}\Big]^{\frac{1+\delta}{2+\delta}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[B^{4+\delta}\big]^{\frac{1}{2+\delta}}\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[A^{2}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[B^{2}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[B^{4+\delta}\big]^{\frac{1}{2+\delta}},

which obtained by twice use of Hölder’s inequality.
To control the term
R_{\textup{III}a}
we consider cases (a) and (b) separately.

Case (a):

As
(x,z)\mapsto\varphi_{r}(x,z)
carries no
x
-dependence it follows
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\varphi_{r}(X,Z)\,|\,\hat{\eta},Z]=\varphi_{r}(X,Z)
and so

\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\varphi_{r}(X,Z)\xi\,|\,\hat{\eta},Z\big]=\varphi_{r}(X,Z)\,\mathbb{E}_{P}[\xi\,|\,Z]=0,

and thus
R_{\textup{III}a}=0
.

Case (b):

By the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality

\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\varphi_{r}(X,Z)\xi\,|\,\hat{\eta},Z\big]^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\varphi_{r}(X,Z)^{2}\,|\,\hat{\eta},Z\big]\,\mathrm{Var}_{P}(X\,|\,Z)\leq C_{g}^{2}C_{\sigma},

almost surely. Thus

\big|R_{\textup{III}a}\big|\leq C_{g}C_{\sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\}^{2}\,|\,\hat{f}\big]=o_{\mathcal{P}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})

Therefore, in both cases (a) and (b),
R_{\textup{III}a}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
and so alongside
R_{\textup{III}b}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
it follows
R_{\textup{III}}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
.

We have shown that
R_{\textup{I}},R_{\textup{II}},R_{\textup{III}}=o_{\mathcal{P}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
and thus

\sup_{r\in[0,1]}\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\{\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z)\}^{\top}\mathbb{E}\big[\nabla_{\eta(z)}^{2}\psi_{1}\big(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z)+r\{\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z)\}\big)\,|\,\hat{\eta},Z\big]\\
\cdot\{\hat{\eta}(Z)-\eta_{P}(Z)\}\Big]=o_{\mathcal{P}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

as required.
∎

The conditions of Proposition
15
are rather involved, but provide general conditions under which the doubly robust condition
(M2)
holds. The assumptions required for (b) are all relatively weak boundedness conditions. In contrast case (a) imposes rather strong assumptions on the model; we show these indeed hold for two commonly used cases of the generalised partially linear model.

Corollary 16
.

The results of Proposition
15
hold for the generalised partially linear model (
9
) with assumption (a) replaced with either of the following:

(a.i)

The partially linear model holds i.e.
g(\mu)=\mu
.

(a.ii)

The generalised partially linear model with log link holds i.e.
g(\mu)=\log\mu
, with additionally
\hat{f}(Z)\geq f_{P}(Z)-C_{f}
almost surely, for some positive constant
C_{f}
.

Proof.

In the case of the generalised partially linear model

\mu(X,Z;\theta_{P},f_{P}(Z))=g^{-1}(X\theta_{P}+f_{P}(Z)),

and so

\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z)=g^{-1}(X\theta_{P}+f_{P}(Z)+r\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\})

Direct calculations show that

\displaystyle\varphi_{r}(X,Z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{g^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z))}{\{g^{\prime}(\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z))\}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{g^{\prime\prime\prime}(\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z))g^{\prime}(\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z))-\{g^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z))\}^{2}}{\{g^{\prime}(\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z))\}^{3}}\big(\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z)-\tilde{\mu}_{0}(X,Z)\big),

\displaystyle\zeta_{r}(X,Z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{g^{\prime\prime}(\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z))}{g^{\prime}(\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z))}\big(\tilde{\mu}_{r}(X,Z)-\tilde{\mu}_{0}(X,Z)\big),

and so

\displaystyle\varphi_{r}(X,Z)=\begin{cases}0&\;\text{when $g=\text{id}$ (a.i)}\\
2-\exp(-r\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\})&\;\text{when $g=\log$ (a.ii)}\end{cases},

\displaystyle\zeta_{r}(X,Z)=\begin{cases}0&\;\text{when $g=\text{id}$ (a.i)}\\
1-\exp(-r\{\hat{f}(Z)-f_{P}(Z)\})&\;\text{when $g=\log$ (a.ii)}\end{cases}.

In both cases it is then easy to see that the condition of (a) hold i.e.
\varphi_{r}(x,z)
and
\zeta_{r}(x,z)
carry no
x
-dependence. Further,

\big|\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\varphi_{r}(X,Z)\,|\,\hat{f},Z\big]\big|\vee\big|\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\zeta_{r}(X,Z)\,|\,\hat{f},Z\big]\big|\begin{cases}=0&\;\text{when $g=\text{id}$ (a.i)}\\
\leq 2\vee|\exp(C_{f})-1|&\;\text{when $g=\text{id}$ (a.ii)}\end{cases}.

∎

Appendix E
Supplementary Information for Section
4

In this section we cover supplementary material regarding the ROSE random forests explored in Section
4
. In Appendix
E.1
the optimal ROSE weights are verified to take the form (
26
). Appendix
E.2
proves the result of Theorem
4
. In Appendix
E.3
we briefly discuss favourable computational properties of ROSE random forests, and prove consistency in Appendix
E.4
. In Appendix
E.5
we generalised the ROSE random forest presented in Appendix
4.2
for
J=1
to accommodate
J>1
.

E.1
Comments on ROSE weighting scheme

We prove that the weights (
26
) minimise the sandwich loss (
25
) as claimed.

Proposition 17
.

The weighting scheme
w_{P}^{\mathrm{(rose)}}=(w_{P,1}^{\mathrm{(rose)}},\ldots,w_{P,J}^{\mathrm{(rose)}})
that minimises the sandwich loss

L_{P,\mathrm{SL}}(w):=\;\frac{\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}w_{j}(Z)\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))\big)^{2}\right]}{\big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[w_{j}(Z)\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))\big]\big)^{2}}.

is (up to an arbitrary positive constant of proportionality) given by

\displaystyle w_{P,j}^{\mathrm{(rose)}}(Z):=e_{j}^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\bm{\psi}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))\bm{\psi}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))^{\top}\,|\,Z\big]^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\bm{\partial_{\theta}\psi}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))\,|\,Z\big],

\displaystyle\bm{\psi}=(\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\ldots,\psi_{J}),\quad\text{where}\;\;\bm{\partial_{\theta}\psi}=(\partial_{\theta}\psi_{1},\partial_{\theta}\psi_{2},\ldots,\partial_{\theta}\psi_{J}).

Proof.

We notate the vector function
\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{R}^{J}
by
w=(w_{1},w_{2},\ldots,w_{J})
, and define the matrix and vector functions
A:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{R}^{J\times J}
and
b:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{R}^{J}
,

\displaystyle A_{P}(Z)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\bm{\psi}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))\bm{\psi}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))^{\top}\,|\,Z\big],

\displaystyle b_{P}(Z)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\bm{\partial_{\theta}\psi}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))\,|\,Z\big].

Then

L_{P,\mathrm{SL}}(w):=\;\frac{\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}w_{j}(Z)\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))\big)^{2}\right]}{\big(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[w_{j}(Z)\partial_{\theta}\psi_{j}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))\big]\big)^{2}}=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\big(\bm{\psi}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))^{\top}w(Z)\big)^{2}\Big]}{\big(\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\bm{\partial_{\theta}\psi}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))^{\top}w(Z)\big]\big)^{2}}\\
=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[w(Z)^{\top}A_{P}(Z)w(Z)\big]}{\big(\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[b_{P}(Z)^{\top}w(Z)\big]\big)^{2}}\geq\big(\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[b_{P}(Z)^{\top}A_{P}(Z)^{-1}b_{P}(Z)\big]\big)^{-1}

with equality if and only if

w(Z)\propto\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\bm{\psi}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))\bm{\psi}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))^{\top}\,|\,Z\big]^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\bm{\partial_{\theta}\psi}(S;\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z))\,|\,Z\big].

This follows by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, obtained by noticing that the quadratic

t\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\big\|A_{P}(Z)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(w(Z)-t\,A_{P}(Z)^{-1}b_{P}(Z)\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big],

has non-positive discriminant.

∎

E.2
Proof of Theorem
4

Proof of Theorem
4
.

Define
\xi:=X-m_{0}(Z)
and
\mu_{0}(X,Z):=g^{-1}(X\theta_{0}+f_{0}(Z))
.
In the generalised linear model context of Theorem
4
for any law
P
satisfying
\mathbb{E}_{P}[X\,|\,Z]=m_{0}(Z)
and
\mathbb{E}_{P}[Y\,|\,X,Z]=\mu_{0}(X,Z)
, the function
\psi_{1}
takes the form

\psi_{1}(S;\theta_{0},\eta_{0}(Z))=g^{\prime}(\mu_{0}(X,Z))\,\xi\,(Y-\mu_{0}(X,Z)).

Therefore, as in (
25
), with
w=w_{1}

L_{P,\mathrm{SL}}(w)=\Big(\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[w(Z)\mathrm{Var}_{P}(X\,|\,Z)\big]\Big)^{-2}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[w^{2}(Z)\{g^{\prime}(\mu_{0}(X,Z))\}^{2}\xi^{2}\mathrm{Var}_{P}(Y\,|\,X,Z)\big]\Big),

and so

\displaystyle V_{P,\mathrm{loceff}}

\displaystyle:=\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg[\frac{\mathrm{Var}_{P}(X\,|\,Z)}{\mathbb{E}_{P}[\{g^{\prime}(g^{-1}(X\theta_{0}+f_{0}(Z)))\}^{2}\mathrm{Var}_{P}(Y\,|\,X,Z)\,|\,Z]}\bigg]\bigg)^{-2}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg[\frac{\mathbb{E}_{P}[\xi^{2}\{g^{\prime}(g^{-1}(X\theta_{0}+f_{0}(Z)))\}^{2}\mathrm{Var}_{P}(Y\,|\,X,Z)\,|\,Z]}{\{\mathbb{E}_{P}[\{g^{\prime}(g^{-1}(X\theta_{0}+f_{0}(Z)))\}^{2}\mathrm{Var}_{P}(Y\,|\,X,Z)\,|\,Z]\}^{2}}\bigg]\bigg),

\displaystyle V_{P,\mathrm{rose}}

\displaystyle:=\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg[\frac{\{\mathrm{Var}_{P}(X\,|\,Z)\}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{P}[\xi^{2}\{g^{\prime}(g^{-1}(X\theta_{0}+f_{0}(Z)))\}^{2}\mathrm{Var}_{P}(Y\,|\,X,Z)\,|\,Z]}\bigg]\bigg)^{-1},

\displaystyle V_{P,\mathrm{unw}}

\displaystyle:=\Big(\mathbb{E}_{P}[\mathrm{Var}_{P}(X\,|\,Z)]\Big)^{-2}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{P}[\xi^{2}\{g^{\prime}(g^{-1}(X\theta_{0}+f_{0}(Z)))\}^{2}\mathrm{Var}_{P}(Y\,|\,X,Z)]\Big).

We proceed to construct a distribution
P_{0}
satisfying the properties in Theorem
4
as follows.
Denote the given marginal distribution for
Z
by
P_{Z}
. First fix an arbitrary
\beta\in(0.5,1)
and
\alpha\in[\beta,1)
. As
Z
is almost surely non-constant there exists some measurable space
\mathcal{Z}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{2}:=\mathcal{Z}\backslash\mathcal{Z}_{1}
such that
q_{1}:=\mathbb{P}_{P_{Z}}(Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{1})\in(0,1)
; also define the constant
q_{2}:=1-q_{1}
. Now define the functions

\displaystyle a(Z)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{X\,|\,Z\sim N(m_{0}(Z),\,p(Z)^{-\beta}),\,Z\sim P_{Z}}\big[\{g^{\prime}(g^{-1}(X\theta_{0}+f_{0}(Z)))\}^{-2}\,|\,Z\big],

\displaystyle b(Z)

\displaystyle:=\{g^{\prime}(g^{-1}(m_{0}(Z)\theta_{0}+f_{0}(Z)))\}^{-2},

noting that both are positive-valued, and so the constants

\displaystyle c:=\mathbb{E}_{P_{Z}}[a(Z)\,|\,Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{1}],\qquad c^{\prime}:=\mathbb{E}_{P_{Z}}[b(Z)\,|\,Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{1}],\qquad c^{\prime\prime}:=\mathbb{E}_{P_{Z}}[b(Z)^{-1}\,|\,Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{1}],

\displaystyle m_{2}^{(1)}:=\mathbb{E}_{P_{Z}}[a(Z)^{-1}\,|\,Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{2}],\qquad m_{2}^{(-1)}:=\mathbb{E}_{P_{Z}}[a(Z)\,|\,Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{2}],

are also all positive.
The map

\tilde{\zeta}\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{P_{Z}}\big[\{a(Z)\tilde{\zeta}+b(Z)(1-\tilde{\zeta})\}^{-1}\,|\,Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{1}\big],

is continuous, and so there exists some
\bar{\zeta}>0
such that for all
\tilde{\zeta}\in[0,\bar{\zeta}]

2^{-1}c^{\prime\prime}\leq\mathbb{E}_{P_{Z}}\big[\{a(Z)\tilde{\zeta}+b(Z)(1-\tilde{\zeta})\}^{-1}\,|\,Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{1}\big]\leq 2c^{\prime\prime}.

Take

\zeta:=1\wedge\bar{\zeta}\wedge\big(2^{-1}A^{-1}q_{1}q_{2}c^{\prime\prime}m_{2}^{(-1)}\big)^{\frac{1}{\alpha+\beta-1}}\wedge\big(q_{1}^{-1}q_{2}(c+c^{\prime})^{-1}m_{2}^{(-1)}\big)^{\frac{1}{\alpha-\beta}}\\
\wedge\big(2^{-1}\{2c^{\prime\prime}q_{1}+m_{2}^{(-1)}q_{2}\}^{-2}A^{-1}q_{2}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2(1-\alpha)}},

and
p(z):=\zeta\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{Z}_{1}}(z)+\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{Z}_{2}}(z)
. Define the conditional distribution
P_{X|Z}
on
X\,|\,Z
by

B\,|\,Z\sim\text{Ber}(p(Z)),\qquad X\,|\,(Z,B)\sim N\big(m_{0}(Z),p^{-\beta}(Z)B\big).

(59)

Then define the conditional distribution for
(Y,X)\,|\,Z
by (
59
) and

Y\,|\,(X,Z,B)\sim N\big(g^{-1}(X\theta_{0}+f_{0}(Z)),\,\{g^{\prime}(g^{-1}(X\theta_{0}+f_{0}(Z)))\}^{-2}\bar{\varphi}(Z)p^{-\alpha}(Z)B\big),

where

\bar{\varphi}(Z):=\mathbb{E}_{P_{X|Z}}\big[\{g^{\prime}(g^{-1}(X\theta_{0}+f_{0}(Z)))\}^{-2}\,|\,Z\big]^{-1}

Together with
P_{Z}
, this implies a joint distribution
P_{0}
for the triple
(Y,X,Z)
. It then follows by construction that
\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[Y\,|\,X,Z]=g^{-1}(X\theta_{0}+f_{0}(Z))
and
\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[X\,|\,Z]=m_{0}(Z)
, in addition to

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}_{P_{0}}(X\,|\,Z)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[\mathrm{Var}_{P_{0}}(X\,|\,Z,B)\,|\,Z]+\mathrm{Var}_{P_{0}}(\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[X\,|\,Z,B]\,|\,Z)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[Bp^{-\beta}(Z)\,|\,Z]+\mathrm{Var}_{P_{0}}(\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[X\,|\,Z]\,|\,Z)

\displaystyle=p^{1-\beta}(Z),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}\big[\xi^{2}\{g^{\prime}(g^{-1}(X\theta_{0}+f_{0}(Z)))\}^{2}\mathrm{Var}_{P_{0}}(Y\,|\,X,Z)\,|\,Z\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}\big[\mathrm{Var}_{P_{0}}(X\,|\,Z,B)\bar{\varphi}(Z)p^{-\alpha}(Z)B\,|\,Z\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}\big[B^{2}p^{-\alpha-\beta}(Z)\bar{\varphi}(Z)\,|\,Z\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}\big[Bp^{-\alpha-\beta}(Z)\bar{\varphi}(Z)\,|\,Z\big]

\displaystyle=p^{1-\alpha-\beta}(Z)\bar{\varphi}(Z),

and similarly

\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}\big[\{g^{\prime}(g^{-1}(X\theta_{0}+f_{0}(Z)))\}^{2}\mathrm{Var}_{P_{0}}(Y\,|\,X,Z)\,|\,Z\big]=p^{1-\alpha}(Z)\bar{\varphi}(Z).

Therefore

\displaystyle V_{P_{0},\mathrm{loceff}}

\displaystyle:=\big(\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}\big[p(Z)^{\alpha-\beta}\bar{\varphi}(Z)^{-1}\big]\big)^{-2}\big(\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}\big[p(Z)^{\alpha-\beta-1}\bar{\varphi}(Z)^{-1}\big]\big),

\displaystyle V_{P_{0},\mathrm{rose}}

\displaystyle:=\big(\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}\big[p(Z)^{1+\alpha-\beta}\bar{\varphi}(Z)^{-1}\big]\big)^{-1},

\displaystyle V_{P_{0},\mathrm{unw}}

\displaystyle:=\big(\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}\big[p(Z)^{1-\beta}\big]\big)^{-2}\big(\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}\big[p(Z)^{1-\alpha-\beta}\bar{\varphi}(Z)\big]\big).

Now

\bar{\varphi}(Z)^{-1}=\mathbb{E}_{P_{X|Z}}\big[\{g^{\prime}(g^{-1}(X\theta_{0}+f_{0}(Z)))\}^{-2}\,|\,Z\big]\\
=a(Z)p(Z)+b(Z)(1-p(Z))=\begin{cases}a(Z)&\;\text{if $Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{1}$}\\
a(Z)\zeta+b(Z)(1-\zeta)&\;\text{if $Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{1}$}\end{cases}.

Then define

\displaystyle m_{1}^{(1)}(\zeta):=\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[\bar{\varphi}(Z)\,|\,Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{1}],\qquad m_{2}^{(1)}=\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[\bar{\varphi}(Z)\,|\,Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{2}],

\displaystyle m_{1}^{(-1)}(\zeta):=\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[\bar{\varphi}(Z)^{-1}\,|\,Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{1}],\qquad m_{2}^{(-1)}=\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[\bar{\varphi}(Z)^{-1}\,|\,Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{2}],

all of which we note are positive.
Also note that by construction
m_{1}^{(1)}(\zeta)\in[2^{-1}c^{\prime\prime},\,2c^{\prime\prime}]

and

m_{1}^{(-1)}(\zeta)=\mathbb{E}_{P_{Z}}\big[\bar{\varphi}(Z)^{-1}\,|\,Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{1}\big]=c\,\zeta+c^{\prime}(1-\zeta)

Then

\displaystyle\frac{V_{P_{0},\mathrm{unw}}}{V_{P_{0},\mathrm{rose}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[p^{1-\alpha-\beta}(Z)\bar{\varphi}(Z)]\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[p^{1+\alpha-\beta}(Z)\bar{\varphi}(Z)^{-1}]}{\big\{\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[p^{1-\beta}(Z)]\big\}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\{\zeta^{1-\alpha-\beta}m_{1}^{(1)}(\zeta)q_{1}+m_{2}^{(1)}q_{2}\}\{\zeta^{1+\alpha-\beta}m_{1}^{(-1)}(\zeta)q_{1}+m_{2}^{(-1)}q_{2}\}}{\{\zeta^{1-\beta}q_{1}+q_{2}\}^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq m_{1}^{(1)}(\zeta)m_{2}^{(-1)}q_{1}q_{2}\zeta^{1-\alpha-\beta}

\displaystyle\geq 2^{-1}c^{\prime\prime}m_{2}^{(-1)}q_{1}q_{2}\zeta^{1-\alpha-\beta}

\displaystyle\geq A,

where we make use of the inequalities
\zeta\leq 1
(so that
\zeta^{1-\beta}q_{1}+q_{2}\leq q_{1}+q_{2}=1
),
\zeta\leq\bar{\zeta}
(so that
m_{1}^{(1)}(\zeta)\geq 2^{-1}c^{\prime\prime}
), and
\zeta\leq\big(2^{-1}A^{-1}q_{1}q_{2}c^{\prime\prime}m_{2}^{(-1)}\big)^{\frac{1}{\alpha+\beta-1}}
for the final inequality, noting that
1-\alpha-\beta<0
.

For the ratio
{V_{P_{0},\mathrm{loceff}}}/{V_{P_{0},\mathrm{unw}}}
,

\displaystyle\frac{V_{P_{0},\mathrm{loceff}}}{V_{P_{0},\mathrm{unw}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[p^{\alpha-\beta-1}(Z)\bar{\varphi}(Z)^{-1}]\big\{\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[p^{1-\beta}(Z)]\big\}^{2}}{\big\{\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[p^{\alpha-\beta}(Z)\bar{\varphi}(Z)^{-1}]\big\}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{P_{0}}[p^{1-\alpha-\beta}(Z)\bar{\varphi}(Z)]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\{\zeta^{\alpha-\beta-1}m_{1}^{(1)}q_{1}+m_{2}^{(1)}q_{2}\}\{\zeta^{1-\beta}q_{1}+q_{2}\}^{2}}{\{\zeta^{\alpha-\beta}m_{1}^{(-1)}q_{1}+m_{2}^{(-1)}q_{2}\}^{2}\{\zeta^{1-\alpha-\beta}m_{1}^{(1)}q_{1}+m_{2}^{(1)}q_{2}\}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{q_{2}^{2}}{2\{m_{1}^{(-1)}(\zeta)q_{1}+m_{2}^{(-1)}q_{2}\}^{2}}\,\zeta^{2(\alpha-1)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{q_{2}^{2}}{2\{2c^{\prime\prime}q_{1}+m_{2}^{(-1)}q_{2}\}^{2}}\,\zeta^{2(\alpha-1)}

\displaystyle\geq A,

with the final inequality following as
\zeta\leq\big(2^{-1}\{2c^{\prime\prime}q_{1}+m_{2}^{(-1)}q_{2}\}^{-2}A^{-1}q_{2}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2(1-\alpha)}}
, alongside

\zeta^{\alpha-\beta}m_{1}^{(-1)}(\zeta)q_{1}+m_{2}^{(-1)}q_{2}\leq\zeta^{\alpha-\beta}(c+c^{\prime})q_{1}+m_{2}^{(-1)}q_{2}\leq 2\zeta^{\alpha-\beta}(c+c^{\prime})q_{1},

where in the above we make use of:
\zeta\leq 1
;
\alpha\geq\beta
;
m_{1}^{(1)}(\zeta)=c+c^{\prime}\zeta\leq c+c^{\prime}
; and
\zeta\leq\big(q_{1}^{-1}q_{2}(c+c^{\prime})^{-1}m_{2}^{(-1)}\big)^{\frac{1}{\alpha-\beta}}
.

Thus the required result is obtained;

\frac{V_{P_{0},\mathrm{loceff}}}{V_{P_{0},\mathrm{unw}}}\geq A\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{V_{P_{0},\mathrm{unw}}}{V_{P_{0},\mathrm{rose}}}\geq A.

∎

E.3
Note on computational complexity of ROSE random forests

Selecting the optimal split point for the
m
th iteration of the decision tree in Algorithm
3
requires updating at most
(1-\alpha)^{m-1}nd
goodness-of-fit measures, the set of which can be calculated in
O((1-\alpha)^{m-1}nd)
complexity. Therefore, the computational complexity to build a tree with
M
splitting points is upper bounded by

\sum_{m=1}^{M}(1-\alpha)^{m-1}nd\leq nd\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}(1-\alpha)^{m-1}=\alpha^{-1}nd

E.4
Asymptotic consistency of ROSE random forests (Theorem
5
)

For notational simplicity throughout the following proof we will denote
\hat{w}_{1}^{\mathrm{(rose)}}
by
\hat{w}_{1}
, omitting the superscript. Note in the below, conditioning on
\hat{w}_{1}^{\mathrm{(rose)}}
(i.e. conditioning on the data used for this estimator) is equivalent to conditioning on
S_{\mathcal{I}}:=(S_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}
(i.e. the data used to construct each ROSE decision tree’s splits and evaluations) in addition to
\hat{\eta}
and
\hat{\theta}
(i.e. the data used for the
\eta_{P}
estimator and the pilot
\theta
estimator). Also, for avoidance of doubt, given non-negative functions e.g.
\omega_{ib}
and for any
q\in\mathbb{R}
we notate exponentiation by
\omega_{ib}^{q}(z)=\{\omega_{ib}(z)\}^{q}
.

Proof of Theorem
5
.

Define the ‘residual terms’

\displaystyle R_{i}

\displaystyle:=\psi_{1}^{2}(S_{i};\hat{\theta},\hat{\eta}(Z_{i}))-\psi_{1}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z_{i})),

\displaystyle R_{\theta,i}

\displaystyle:=\partial_{\theta}\psi_{1}(S_{i};\hat{\theta},\hat{\eta}(Z_{i}))-\partial_{\theta}\psi_{1}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z_{i})),

and for each
z\in\mathcal{Z}
define

\displaystyle L_{i}(z)

\displaystyle:=\psi_{1}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z_{i}))-\mathbb{E}_{P}[\psi_{1}^{2}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z_{i}))|Z_{i}=z,\hat{\eta}],

\displaystyle Q_{i}(z)

\displaystyle:=\partial_{\theta}\psi_{1}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z_{i}))-\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\partial_{\theta}\psi_{1}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z_{i}))|Z_{i}=z,\hat{\eta}\right],

\displaystyle U_{\theta}(z)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\partial_{\theta}\psi_{1}(S_{i};\theta_{P},\eta_{P}(Z_{i}))|Z_{i}=z\right].

Also recall
\omega_{ib}(z)
as defined in Algorithm
3
and let

\omega_{i}(z):=\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\omega_{ib}(z)

. Then

\big(\hat{w}_{1}(z)-w_{P,1}^{\mathrm{(rose)}}(z)\big)^{2}=\frac{\Delta^{2}(z)}{U^{2}(z)\big(U(z)+\tilde{\Delta}(z)\big)^{2}},

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}(z)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}(z)\big\{L_{i}(z)+R_{i}\big\},

\displaystyle\Delta(z)

\displaystyle:=U(z)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}(z)\big\{Q_{i}(z)+R_{\theta,i}\big\}-U_{\theta}(z)\tilde{\Delta}(z).

Without loss of generality take
c_{w}\geq c_{1}^{-1}C_{2}
; otherwise replace
c_{w}
with
c_{w}\vee(c_{1}^{-1}C_{2})
. Then for an arbitrary
\kappa\in(0,1)
and
P\in\mathcal{P}
,

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big(\hat{w}_{1}(Z)-w_{P,1}^{\mathrm{(rose)}}(Z)\big)^{2}\,\Big|\,\hat{w}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big(\hat{w}_{1}(Z)-w_{P,1}^{\mathrm{(rose)}}(Z)\big)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\left|\tilde{\Delta}(Z)\right|<(1-\kappa)|U(Z)|\right\}}\,\Big|\,\hat{w}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big(\hat{w}_{1}(Z)-w_{P,1}^{\mathrm{(rose)}}(Z)\big)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\left|\tilde{\Delta}(Z)\right|\geq(1-\kappa)|U(Z)|\right\}}\,\Big|\,\hat{w}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{w}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\big|\hat{w}_{1}(Z)-w_{P,1}^{\mathrm{(rose)}}(Z)\big|\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\left|\tilde{\Delta}(Z)\right|<(1-\kappa)|U(Z)|\right\}}\,\Big|\,\hat{w}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+4c_{w}^{2}\mathbb{P}_{P}\left(\big|\tilde{\Delta}(Z)\big|>(1-\kappa)|U(Z)|\,\Big|\,\hat{w}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2c_{w}}{\kappa}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\frac{|\Delta(Z)|}{U^{2}(Z)}\,\bigg|\,\hat{w}_{1}\right]+4c_{w}^{2}\mathbb{P}_{P}\left(\big|\tilde{\Delta}(Z)\big|>(1-\kappa)|U(Z)|\,\Big|\,\hat{w}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2c_{w}}{\kappa}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\frac{|\Delta(Z)|}{U^{2}(Z)}\,\bigg|\,\hat{w}_{1}\right]+\frac{4c_{w}^{2}}{1-\kappa}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\frac{|\tilde{\Delta}(Z)|}{|U(Z)|}\,\bigg|\,\hat{w}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2c_{w}}{c_{1}^{2}\kappa}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[|\Delta(Z)|\,\big|\,\hat{w}_{1}\big]+\frac{4c_{w}^{2}}{c_{1}(1-\kappa)}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[|\tilde{\Delta}(Z)|\,\big|\,\hat{w}_{1}\big],

with the first inequality following by the Cauchy–Schwarz and triangle inequalities, the second by Markov’s inequality, and the third by Assumption
(M7)
. Therefore, it suffices to show that
\mathbb{E}_{P}[|\Delta(Z)|\,|\,\hat{w}_{1}]
and
\mathbb{E}_{P}[|\tilde{\Delta}(Z)|\,|\,\hat{w}_{1}]
are each
o_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
. By the triangle inequality

\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[|\Delta(X)|\,\big|\,\hat{w}_{1}\big]\leq M\Bigg(\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg[\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}(Z)Q_{i}(Z_{i})\bigg|\,\bigg|\,\hat{w}_{1}\bigg]}_{=:\mathcal{Q}_{\textup{I}}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg[\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}(Z)L_{i}(Z_{i})\bigg|\,\bigg|\,\hat{w}_{1}\bigg]}_{{}_{=:\mathcal{L}_{\textup{I}}}}\\
+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg[\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}(Z)\big(Q_{i}(Z)-Q_{i}(Z_{i})\big)\bigg|\,\bigg|\,\hat{w}_{1}\bigg]}_{=:\mathcal{Q}_{\textup{II}}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg[\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}(Z)\big(L_{i}(Z)-L_{i}(Z_{i})\big)\bigg|\,\bigg|\,\hat{w}_{1}\bigg]}_{=:\mathcal{L}_{\textup{II}}}\\
+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg[\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}(Z)R_{i}\bigg|\,\bigg|\,\hat{w}_{1}\bigg]}_{=:\mathcal{R}_{\textup{II}}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigg[\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}(Z)R_{\theta,i}\bigg|\,\bigg|\,\hat{w}_{1}\bigg]}_{=:\mathcal{R}_{\textup{I}}},

(60)

and
